His political theory centred in the <a href="/wiki/Glorious_Revolution" title="Glorious Revolution">revolution of 1688</a>, and in the clique of aristocratic families to whom the country owed loyalty in return for something like the <i><span title="French-language text"><i lang="fr"><a href="/wiki/Charter_of_1814" title="Charter of 1814">charte octroyée</a></i></span></i> of the reform bill.</p><div class="templatequotecite">—&#8202;<cite><a href="#CITEREFWoodward1962">Woodward 1962</a>, p.&#160;100</cite></div></blockquote><p>  Nevertheless, Russell led his Whig party into support for reform; he was the principal architect of the <a href="/wiki/Reform_Act_1832" title="Reform Act 1832">Great Reform Act of 1832</a>.
</p><p>He was succeeded as Liberal leader by former Peelite <a href="/wiki/William_Ewart_Gladstone" title="William Ewart Gladstone">William Gladstone</a>, and was thus the last true Whig to serve as Prime Minister. Generally taken as the model for <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Trollope" title="Anthony Trollope">Anthony Trollope</a>'s Mr. Mildmay, aspects of his character may also have suggested those of <a href="/wiki/Plantagenet_Palliser" class="mw-redirect" title="Plantagenet Palliser">Plantagenet Palliser</a>. An ideal statesman, said Trollope, should have "unblemished, unextinguishable, inexhaustible love of country.... But he should also be scrupulous, and, as being scrupulous, weak."<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/1832_Reform_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="1832 Reform Act">1832 Reform Act</a> and extension of the franchise to British cities are partly attributed to his efforts. He also worked for emancipation, leading the attack on the <a href="/wiki/Test_Act" class="mw-redirect" title="Test Act">Test</a> and <a href="/wiki/Corporation_Act_1661" title="Corporation Act 1661">Corporation acts</a>, which were repealed in 1828, as well as towards legislation limiting working hours in factories in the <a href="/wiki/Factories_Act_1847" title="Factories Act 1847">1847 Factory Act</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Public_Health_Act_1848" class="mw-redirect" title="Public Health Act 1848">Public Health Act of 1848</a>.
</p><p>His government's approach to dealing with the Great Irish Famine is now widely condemned as counterproductive, ill-informed and disastrous. Russell himself was sympathetic to the plight of the Irish poor, and many of his relief proposals were blocked by his cabinet or by the British Parliament.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEScherer1999158_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEScherer1999158-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Queen_Victoria" title="Queen Victoria">Queen Victoria</a>'s attitude toward Russell was coloured by his role in the Aberdeen administration. On his death in 1878 her journal records that he was "a man of much talent, who leaves a name behind him, kind, &amp; good, with a great knowledge of the constitution, who behaved very well, on many trying occasions; but he was impulsive, very selfish (as shown on many occasions, especially during Ld Aberdeen's administration) vain, &amp; often reckless &amp; imprudent."
</p><p>A public house in Bloomsbury, large parts of which are still owned by the Bedford Estate, is named after Russell, located on Marchmont Street.
</p><p>The town of <a href="/wiki/Russell,_New_Zealand" title="Russell, New Zealand">Russell</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Northland_Region" title="Northland Region">Northland Region</a> of New Zealand, was named in honour of him as the then Secretary of State for the Colonies.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Literature">Literature</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Russell,_1st_Earl_Russell&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Literature">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Original_works">Original works</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Russell,_1st_Earl_Russell&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Original works">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Russell published numerous books and essays over the course of his life, especially during periods out of office. He principally wrote on politics and history, but also turned his hand to a variety of other topics and genres. His published works include:
</p>
<ul><li><i>The Life of <a href="/wiki/William_Russell,_Lord_Russell" title="William Russell, Lord Russell">William Lord Russell</a></i> (1819) - a biography of his famous ancestor.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERussell1820_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussell1820-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>Essays and Sketches of Life and Character by a Gentleman who has left his lodgings</i> (1820) - a series of social and cultural commentaries ostensibly found in a missing lodger's rooms, published anonymously.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERussell1820b_78-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERussell1820b-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup></li>
<li><i>An Essay on the History of the English Government and Constitution, from the reign of <a href="/wiki/Henry_VII_of_England" title="Henry VII of England">Henry VII</a>. to the present time</i> (1821)</li>
<li><i>The Nun of Arrouca: a Tale</i> (1822) - a romantic novel set in Portugal during the Peninsular War.</li>
<li><i>Don Carlos: or, Persecution. A tragedy, in five acts</i> (1822) - a blank verse play on the same subject as the <a href="/wiki/Don_Carlos_(play)" title="Don Carlos (play)">play of the same title</a> by <a href="/wiki/Friedrich_Schiller" title="Friedrich Schiller">Friedrich Schiller</a>.</li>
<li><i>Memoirs of the Affairs of Europe from the <a href="/wiki/Peace_of_Utrecht" title="Peace of Utrecht">Peace of Utrecht</a></i> (1824) - a second volume appeared in 1829.</li>
<li><i>The Establishment of the Turks in Europe, An Historical Discourse</i> (1828)</li>
<li><i>The Causes of the French Revolution</i> (1832) <figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_(BM_1859,0625.112).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_%28BM_1859%2C0625.112%29.jpg/300px-Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_%28BM_1859%2C0625.112%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="231" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_%28BM_1859%2C0625.112%29.jpg/450px-Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_%28BM_1859%2C0625.112%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/84/Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_%28BM_1859%2C0625.112%29.jpg/600px-Author_of_the_%22Memoirs_of_the_Affairs_of_Europe.%22_%28BM_1859%2C0625.112%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2371" data-file-height="1824" /></a><figcaption>"The Author of Memoirs of the Affairs of Europe" - an illustration of Russell from <a href="/wiki/Fraser%27s_Magazine" title="Fraser&#39;s Magazine">Fraser's Magazine</a>, 1831</figcaption></figure></li>
<li><i>Adventures in the Moon, and Other Worlds</i> (1836) - a collection of <a href="/wiki/Fantasy_literature" title="Fantasy literature">fantasy</a> short stories, published anonymously.</li>
<li><i>The Life and Times of <a href="/wiki/Charles_James_Fox" title="Charles James Fox">Charles James Fox</a></i> (1859-1866) - a three volume biography of Russell's political hero.</li>
<li><i>Essays on the Rise and Progress of the Christian Religion in the West of Europe, from the reign of <a href="/wiki/Tiberius" title="Tiberius">Tiberius</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Trent" title="Council of Trent">Council of Trent</a></i> (1871)</li>
<li><i>The Foreign Policy of England 1570-1870, An Historical Essay</i> (1871)</li>
<li><i>Recollections and Suggestions 1813-1873</i> (1875) - Russell's political memoir.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="As_editor">As editor</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Russell,_1st_Earl_Russell&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: As editor">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<ul><li><i>Correspondence of <a href="/wiki/John_Russell,_4th_Duke_of_Bedford" title="John Russell, 4th Duke of Bedford">John, Fourth Duke of Bedford</a></i> - in three volumes, published between 1842 and 1846.</li>
<li><i>Memoirs, Journal, and Correspondence of <a href="/wiki/Thomas_Moore" title="Thomas Moore">Thomas Moore</a></i> - in eight volumes, published between 1853 and 1856. Russell was Moore's literary executor and published his papers in accordance with his late friend's wishes.</li>
<li><i>Memorials and correspondence of Charles James Fox</i> - in four volumes, published between 1853 and 1857.</li></ul>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Dedications">Dedications</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Russell,_1st_Earl_Russell&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Dedications">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p><i><a href="/wiki/A_Tale_of_Two_Cities" title="A Tale of Two Cities">A Tale of Two Cities</a></i> by <a href="/wiki/Charles_Dickens" title="Charles Dickens">Charles Dickens</a> was dedicated to Lord John Russell, "In remembrance of many public services and private kindnesses."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDickens1866iii_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDickens1866iii-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> In speech given in 1869, Dickens remarked of Russell that "there is no man in England whom I respect more in his public capacity, whom I love more in his private capacity."<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDickens1906290_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDickens1906290-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Ancestry">Ancestry</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=John_Russell,_1st_Earl_Russell&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Ancestry">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r886387087">.mw-parser-output table.ahnentafel{border-collapse:separate;border-spacing:0;line-height:130%}.mw-parser-output .ahnentafel tr{text-align:center}.mw-parser-output .ahnentafel-t{border-top:#000 solid 1px;border-left:#000 solid 1px}.mw-parser-output .ahnentafel-b{border-bottom:#000 solid 1px;border-left:#000 solid 1px}</style><div class="noresize"><table class="collapsible collapsed" style="margin:0.3em auto;clear:none;min-width:60em;width:auto;font-size:88%;border:1px solid #aaa"><tbody><tr><th style="padding:0.2em 0.3em 0.2em 4.3em;background:none;width:auto">Ancestors of John Russell, 1st Earl Russell</th></tr><tr><td style="text-align:center"><table class="ahnentafel" style="margin-left:auto; <ul><li>1834–1836	<a href="/wiki/Henry_Petty-Fitzmaurice,_3rd_Marquess_of_Lansdowne" title="Henry Petty-Fitzmaurice, 3rd Marquess of Lansdowne">The Marquess of Lansdowne</a></li>
